above. The case
(z,[v,x])
, finally, is trivial because
[v,x]=0
.

(8)

v=(\lambda,a,a^{\prime},\mu)_{-}
,
w=\mathbf{d}_{e,e^{\prime}}
.
By
Corollary

A.6
, we can skip this case, as it will follow from
Item

11
.
(Notice that the case
v=(\lambda,a,a^{\prime},\mu)_{-}
,
w=e^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
holds by
Remark

A.4
.)

(9)

v=(\lambda,a,a^{\prime},\mu)_{-}
,
w=\xi
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{3}[v,w]

\displaystyle=-\mu N(b)y

\displaystyle[\delta_{3}v,w]

\displaystyle=2\mu N(b)y

\displaystyle[\delta_{2}v,\delta_{1}w]

\displaystyle=-3\mu N(b)y

\displaystyle[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{2}w]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle[v,\delta_{3}w]

\displaystyle=0.

(10)

v=(\lambda,a,a^{\prime},\mu)_{-}
,
w=\zeta
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{3}[v,w]

\displaystyle=\mu N(b)y

\displaystyle[\delta_{3}v,w]

\displaystyle=\mu N(b)y

\displaystyle[\delta_{2}v,\delta_{1}w]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{2}w]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle[v,\delta_{3}w]

\displaystyle=0.

(11)

v=(\lambda,a,a^{\prime},\mu)_{-}
,
w=c\in J
. Then

\displaystyle\delta_{3}[v,w]

\displaystyle=T(c,a^{\prime})N(b)y

\displaystyle[\delta_{3}v,w]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle[\delta_{2}v,\delta_{1}w]

\displaystyle=T(c,a^{\prime})N(b)y-T(b,a^{\prime})T(c,b^{\sharp})y

\displaystyle[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{2}w]

\displaystyle=T(b,a^{\prime})T(c,b^{\sharp})y

\displaystyle[v,\delta_{3}w]

\displaystyle=0,

where we have used
Lemma

2.8
Item

8
in the computation of
[\delta_{2}v,\delta_{1}w]
.

The case
\ell=4
.

Notice that
\delta_{4}=0
, so we have to show that

(*)_{4}

0=[\delta_{3}v,\delta_{1}w]+[\delta_{2}v,\delta_{2}w]+[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{3}w]

for all
v,w\in L
.
By
Remark

A.4
, there are not many cases to consider.

(1)

v=x
,
w=(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}
,

(2)

v=(\lambda,a,a^{\prime},\mu)_{-}
,
w=(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}
.

Notice that when
v=x\in L_{-2,-1}
and
w\in L_{0j}
,
Remark

A.4
always applies because
L_{2,-1+j}=0
unless
j=2
, but then
L_{0j}=L_{02}=0
.

(1)

v=x
,
w=(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}
. Then

\displaystyle[\delta_{3}v,\delta_{1}w]

\displaystyle=\bigl(-N(b)T(b,c^{\prime}),\ \rho N(b)b,\ 0,\ 0\bigr)_{+}

\displaystyle[\delta_{2}v,\delta_{2}w]

\displaystyle=\bigl(T(b,c^{\prime})T(b,b^{\sharp})-T(b^{\sharp}\times c,b^{\sharp}),\ -\rho b^{\sharp}\times b^{\sharp},\ 0,\ 0\bigr)_{+}

\displaystyle[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{3}w]

\displaystyle=\bigl(0,\ \rho N(b)b,\ 0,\ 0\bigr)_{+}.

The result now follows since
b^{\sharp}\times b^{\sharp}=2b^{\sharp\sharp}=2N(b)b
and
T(b,b^{\sharp})=3N(b)
.

(2)

v=(\lambda,a,a^{\prime},\mu)_{-}
,
w=(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}
. We could, of course, directly compute that

(
∗
)

4

holds, but this is not necessary by the same argument as before, invoking
Lemma

A.5
.

The case
\ell\geq 5
.

Since
\delta_{j}=0
for all
j\geq 4
, the identities

(
∗
)

ℓ

become trivial as soon as
\ell\geq 7
. For
\ell=5
and
\ell=6
, they simplify to

(*)_{5}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=[\delta_{3}v,\delta_{2}w]+[\delta_{2}v,\delta_{3}w]\quad\text{and}

(*)_{6}

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=[\delta_{3}v,\delta_{3}w],

respectively. By
Remark

A.4
, however, they are trivially satisfied if
v\in L_{i}
and
w\in L_{j}
with
i+j>-3
.
So the only remaining case is
v=x
and
w\in L_{-1}
, but since
L_{-1,2}=0
, this is again dealt with by the same
Remark

A.4
.

This finishes the proof that the maps
e_{(0,b,0,0)_{+}}
are automorphisms of the Lie algebra
L
.

A.2.
The exponential maps
e_{\rho x}

We now show that the maps
e_{\rho x}
from
Definition

7.11
are automorphisms of the Lie algebra
L
. This works in the same way as for the maps
e_{(0,b,0,0)_{+}}
, but the number of cases we have to verify will be much lower. We fix
\rho\in k
, put
\varphi\coloneq e_{\rho x}
and decompose
\varphi=\sum_{\ell=0}^{2}\delta_{\ell}
. Here
\delta_{0}=\id
,
\delta_{1}=\ad_{\rho x}
and
\delta_{2}
is defined by
\delta_{2}(y)=\rho^{2}x
and
\delta_{2}(L_{ij})=0
for all
(i,j)\neq(2,1)
. Further,
\delta_{1}
is trivial on all
L_{ij}
except for the following cases:

\displaystyle\delta_{1}(\xi)=2\rho x,\quad\delta_{1}(\zeta)=\rho x,\quad\delta_{1}\big((\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{+}\big)=-\rho(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{,}\quad\delta_{1}(y)=\rho\xi.

Remark A.7
.

Similarly to
Remark

A.4
, we have
\delta_{\ell}(L_{i,j})=L_{i-2\ell,j-\ell}
for all
i,j,\ell
. Hence

(
∗
)

ℓ

is trivially satisfied for all
v\in L_{ij}
,
w\in L_{rs}
with
L_{i+r-2\ell,j+s-\ell}=0
.

In order to verify

(*)_{2}

\delta_{2}[v,w]=[\delta_{2}v,w]+[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{1}w]+[v,\delta_{2}w],

only the following cases have to be considered by
Remark

A.7
.

(1)

v\in L_{0}
,
w=y
.

(2)

v=(0,0,0,\mu)_{+}
,
w=y
.

(3)

v=(0,0,a^{\prime},0)_{+}
,
w=(0,0,b^{\prime},0)_{+}
.

(4)

v=(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{+}
,
w=y
.

(5)

v=y
,
w=y
.

In case
Item

4
, we compute that

\displaystyle[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{1}w]

\displaystyle=[-\rho(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{-},\rho\xi]=-\rho^{2}(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{-},

\displaystyle[v,\delta_{2}w]

\displaystyle=[(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{+},\rho^{2}x]=\rho^{2}(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{-}

and
\delta_{2}[v,w]=[\delta_{2}v,w]=[v,\delta_{2}w]=0
. In case
Item

5
, we have
\delta_{2}[v,w]=0=[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{1}w]
and
[\delta_{2}v,w]=-[v,\delta_{2}w]
because
v=w
. In all remaining cases,

\delta_{2}[v,w]=[\delta_{2}v,w]=[\delta_{1}v,\delta_{1}w]=[v,\delta_{2}w]=0.

Thus
(*)_{2}
is satisfied.

We now turn to
(*)_{3}
. Using
Remark

A.7
, we can omit all cases except for
v=y=w
, and this case is trivial by the same arguments as for
(*)_{2}
. The relations
(*)_{\ell}
for
\ell\geq 4
hold by
Remark

A.7
as well. We conclude that
e_{\rho x}
is indeed an automorphism of
L
.

Appendix B
Weyl elements in the
G_{2}
-grading

We can summarize the results of
Appendix

A
as follows.

Lemma B.1
.

We have
e_{\lambda x}\in\End(L)
for all
\lambda\in k
.

Lemma B.2
.

We have
e_{(0,b,0,0)_{+}}\in\End(L)
for all
b\in J
.

The goal of this appendix is to show that all the remaining maps defined in
Definitions

7.11
,
7.12
,
7.13
and
7.14
can be expressed as the conjugate of
e_{\lambda x}
or
e_{(0,b,0,0)_{+}}
by a suitable product of the reflections defined in
Section

5
. In particular, we infer that all these maps are compatible with the Lie bracket, and hence lie in
\End(L)
. Once this is done, only a few additional computations are required to show that the reflections are actually Weyl elements in the sense of
Definition

1.5
Item

3
.

Lemma B.3
.

For all
\lambda\in k
, we have
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{\lambda y}
. In particular,
e_{\lambda y}\in\End(L)
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in k
. Using the formulas in
Definitions

5.8
,
5.17
and
7.11
, we can compute that
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}
acts as the identity on
L_{1}+L_{2}
and on
\mathbf{d}_{c,c^{\prime}}
for all
c\in J
,
c^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
, and that it acts on the remaining generators of the
k
-modules
L
as follows:

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto x-\lambda\xi+\lambda^{2}y,

\displaystyle(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}+\lambda(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{+},

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto\xi-2\lambda y,

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle\mapsto\zeta-\lambda y.

Comparing the results of our computation to the definition of
e_{\lambda y}
, we conclude that
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{\lambda y}
. By
Lemmas

B.1
and
5.15
, it follows that
e_{\lambda y}\in\End(L)
.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

For all
\lambda\in k
, we have
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{\lambda x}
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in k
. It follows from
Lemma

B.3
that
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-2}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{2}
. Since
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{2}
is the parity automorphism of the grading
(L_{i})_{-2\leq i\leq 2}
by
Remark

5.12
, the assertion follows from
Lemma

7.7
.
∎

Lemma B.5
.

For all
a\in J
and
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
, we have
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{a}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{a}
and
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{a^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{a^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to check that
e_{a}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}\circ e_{a}
and
e_{a^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}\circ e_{a^{\prime}}
, which is straightforward. The main argument is the observation that the formulas for the action of
e_{a}
on
L_{1}
and
L_{-1}
are identical, and similarly for
e_{a^{\prime}}
.
∎

Lemma B.6
.

We have
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{y}\circ e_{x}\circ e_{y}=e_{x}\circ e_{y}\circ e_{x}
.

Proof.

As in
Lemma

B.3
, we can compute that

\displaystyle e_{y}\circ e_{x}\circ e_{y}(x)

\displaystyle=y,\qquad e_{y}\circ e_{x}\circ e_{y}\big((\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{+}\big)=-(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}

for all
\nu,\rho\in k
,
c\in J
and
c^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
. Since all the maps
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}
,
e_{x}
and
e_{y}
lie in
\End(L)
, it follows from
Remark

7.19
that
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{y}\circ e_{x}\circ e_{y}
. This implies that

\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}}=e_{y}^{\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}}\circ e_{x}^{\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}}\circ e_{y}^{\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}}=e_{x}\circ e_{y}\circ e_{x}

by
Lemmas

B.3
and
B.4
.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

For all
\lambda\in k
, we have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(-\lambda,0,0,0)_{-}}
. In particular,
e_{(\lambda,0,0,0)_{-}}\in\End(L)
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in k
. Similarly as in the proof of
Lemma

B.3
, we can compute that
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
acts on the generators of the
k
-module
L
as follows:

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle\mapsto y+(\lambda,0,0,0)_{-},\qquad x\mapsto x,

\displaystyle c^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto c^{\prime},

\displaystyle(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}+\lambda\rho x,

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto\xi+(-\lambda,0,0,0)_{-},

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{c,c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{d}_{c,c^{\prime}}+\big(-T(c,c^{\prime})\lambda,0,0,0\big)_{-},

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle\mapsto\zeta+(-2\lambda,0,0,0)_{-},

\displaystyle(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{+}+\lambda c^{\prime}-\lambda\rho\zeta+(\lambda^{2}\rho,0,0,0)_{-},

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\mapsto c+(0,\lambda c,0,0)_{-}

for all
\nu,\rho\in k
,
c\in J
,
c^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
. We conclude that
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(-\lambda,0,0,0)_{-}}
. By
Lemmas

B.1
and
5.15
, it follows that
e_{(\lambda,0,0,0)_{-}}\in\End(L)
.
∎

Lemma B.8
.

For all
\lambda\in k
, we have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,0,0,-\lambda)_{+}}
. In particular,
e_{(0,0,0,\lambda)_{+}}\in\End(L)
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in k
. Then
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,0,0,-\lambda)_{+}}
acts on
L
as follows:

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto x+(0,0,0,-\lambda)_{-},

\displaystyle(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{-}+\lambda\nu\zeta+(0,0,0,\lambda^{2}\nu)_{+}-\lambda c,

\displaystyle c^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto c^{\prime}+(0,0,-\lambda c^{\prime},0)_{+},

\displaystyle(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{+}-\lambda\nu y,

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\mapsto\xi+(0,0,0,\lambda)_{+},

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{c,c^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{d}_{c,c^{\prime}}+\big(0,0,0,T(c,c^{\prime})\lambda\big)_{+},

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle\mapsto\zeta+(0,0,0,2\lambda)_{+},

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\mapsto c,\qquad y\mapsto y

for all
\nu,\rho\in k
,
c\in J
,
c^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
. We conclude that
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,0,0,-\lambda)_{+}}
. By
Lemmas

B.3
and
5.15
, it follows that
e_{(0,0,0,\lambda)_{+}}\in\End(L)
.
∎

Lemma B.9
.

(1)

Let
\lambda,\mu\in k
,
b\in J
,
b^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
such that at most one of
\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu
is non-zero. Then
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{-}}
.

(2)

We have
e_{(\lambda,0,0,0)_{\varepsilon}},e_{(0,b,0,0)_{\varepsilon}},e_{(0,0,0,\lambda)_{\varepsilon}}\in\End(L)
for all
\lambda\in k
,
b\in J
and
\varepsilon\in\{\mathord{\pm}\}
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in k
,
b\in J
,
b^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
such that at most one of
\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu
is non-zero. Then a similar computation as before shows that
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}
agrees with
e_{(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{-}}
on the usual set of generators of the
k
-module
L
.
The final assertion follows from
Lemmas

B.1
,
B.2
,
B.7
and
B.8
.
∎

Lemma B.10
.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in k
,
b\in J
,
b^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
such that at most one of
\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu
is non-zero. Then
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=e_{-(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{+}}
.

Proof.

We have
\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-1}\circ e_{(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}=\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{-2}\circ e_{(\lambda,b,b^{\prime},\mu)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{2}
by
Lemma

B.9
. Thus the assertion follows from
Remarks

5.13
and
7.7
.
∎

The following
Lemmas

B.11
and
B.12
follow from the same kind of computations as before.

Lemma B.11
.

For all
a\in J
, we have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,a,0,0)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{-a}
. In particular,
e_{a}\in\End(L)
.

Lemma B.12
.

For all
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
, we have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0,a^{\prime},0)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{-a^{\prime}}
.

Lemma B.13
.

Using the notation from
Remark

7.16
, we have

(\psi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J^{\prime},J)})^{-1}\circ e_{(0,a,0,0)_{+}}^{(J,J^{\prime})}\circ\psi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J^{\prime},J)}=e_{-a}^{(J^{\prime},J)}

for all
a\in J
. In particular,
e_{a^{\prime}}^{(J,J^{\prime})}\in\End(L(J,J^{\prime}))
for all
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from the same kind of computation as before. By
Lemmas

B.2
and
5.15
, it follows that for all cubic norm pairs
(J,J^{\prime})
and all
a\in J
, the map
e_{a}^{(J^{\prime},J)}
is an endomorphism of
L(J^{\prime},J)
. Applying this observation to the cubic norm pair
(J^{\prime},J)
, the second assertion follows.
∎

The results in this section up to this point can be summarized as follows.

Proposition B.14
.

(1)

Let
\lambda\in k
and

e\in\{e_{\lambda x},e_{(\lambda,0,0,0)_{\pm}},e_{(0,0,0,\lambda)_{\pm}},e_{\lambda y}\}.

Then there exist
n\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,
\epsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
and
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}\in\{\varphi_{\leftrightarrow},\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}\}
such that
e=\varphi^{-1}\circ e_{\epsilon\lambda x}\circ\varphi
where
\varphi\coloneq\varphi_{1}\cdots\varphi_{n}
(which should be interpreted as
\id_{L}
if
n=0
). In particular,
e\in\End(L)
.

(2)

Let
a\in J
and
e\in\{e_{a},e_{(0,a,0,0)_{\pm}}\}
. Then there exist
\varphi\in\{\varphi_{\leftrightarrow},\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}},\id_{L}\}
and
\epsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
such that
e=\varphi^{-1}\circ e_{(0,\epsilon a,0,0)_{+}}\circ\varphi
. In particular,
e\in\End(L)
.

(3)

Let
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
and

e\in\{e_{a^{\prime}}^{(J,J^{\prime})},e_{(0,0,a^{\prime},0)_{\pm}}^{(J,J^{\prime})}\}.

Then there exist

\varphi^{(J^{\prime},J)}\in\{\id_{L},\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J,J^{\prime})}\circ\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}^{(J,J^{\prime})}\}

and
\epsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
such that

(\varphi^{(J,J^{\prime})})^{-1}\circ e\circ\varphi^{(J,J^{\prime})}=(\psi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J,J^{\prime})})^{-1}\circ e_{(0,\epsilon a^{\prime},0,0)}^{(J^{\prime},J)}\circ\psi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J,J^{\prime})}.

In particular,
e\in\End(L)
.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from
Lemmas

B.1
,
B.3
,
B.7
,
B.8
,
B.9
and
B.10
. The second assertion follows from
Lemmas

B.2
,
B.9
and
B.11
. Finally, note that

(\psi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J,J^{\prime})})^{-1}\circ e_{(0,\epsilon a^{\prime},0,0)}^{(J^{\prime},J)}\circ\psi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J,J^{\prime})}=e_{-\epsilon a^{\prime}}^{(J,J^{\prime})}

by
Lemma

B.13
and that the left-hand map lies in
\End(L)
by the second assertion. Thus the third assertion follows from
Lemmas

B.12
and
B.9
.
∎

With
Proposition

B.14
established, it now follows from
Proposition

7.20
that
\exp_{\alpha}\colon L_{\alpha}\to\Aut(L)\colon a\mapsto e_{a}
is a well-defined
\alpha
-parametrization in
L
, and we can define root groups
(U_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in G_{2}}
in
\Aut(L)
as in
Definition

7.21
. In the remaining part of this appendix, we will show that these root groups have Weyl elements in the sense of
Definition

1.5
Item

3
. In fact, one half of the work is already done.

Proposition B.15
.

\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}
is a
(2,1)
-Weyl element with respect to
(U_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in G_{2}}
.

Proof.

This follows from
Lemmas

B.6
,
B.3
,
B.4
,
B.9
and
B.10
.
∎

By
Remark

1.8
, it remains to find a
\delta
-Weyl element for a root
\delta
such that
\{(2,1),\delta\}
is a system of simple roots in
G_{2}
. We opt to find a
(-1,-1)
-Weyl element.

Convention B.16
.

From now on, we assume that there exists
b\in J
for which
N(b)
is invertible. We fix such a
b
and put
b^{\prime}\coloneq N(b)^{-1}b^{\sharp}
. By
Remark

2.17
, the pair
(N(b)U_{b^{\prime}},N(b)^{-1}U_{b})
is then a
t
-involution of
(J,J^{\prime})
for
t\coloneq N(b)
, which we denote by
(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}})
.

We aim to show that the reflection
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}\coloneq\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},t)}=\psi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(J^{\prime},J)}\circ L(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},t)
from
Definition

5.18
is a
(-1,-1)
-Weyl element. We begin by showing that it has the desired product decomposition.

Lemma B.17
.

We have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}=e_{(0,0,-b^{\prime},0)_{+}}\circ e_{(0,b,0,0)_{-}}\circ e_{(0,0,-b^{\prime},0)_{+}}
.

Proof.

We compute the action of
g\coloneq e_{(0,0,-b^{\prime},0)_{+}}\circ e_{(0,b,0,0)_{-}}\circ e_{(0,0,-b^{\prime},0)_{+}}
on the generating set

\varphi_{\leftrightarrow}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{45.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}(kx+L_{1})=kx+(0,0,J^{\prime},0)_{-}+J+(k,0,0,k)_{+}

of
L
. Since this computation relies on several identities, we perform it in full detail, with each arrow
“
\mapsto
”
denoting the application of the next consecutive factor of
g
. We will frequently use that
T(b,b^{\sharp})=3N(b)=3t
by Axiom
2.3
Item

3
, that
(b^{\prime})^{\sharp}=t^{-1}b
and
N(b^{\prime})=-t^{-1}
and that
b\times b=2b^{\sharp}
because
\times
is the linearization of
\sharp
. We will also apply
Lemma

2.8
Items

7
and
8
and Axioms
2.3
Items

5
and
6
in many cases.

Each factor of
g
fixes
(k,0,0,0)_{+}
pointwise. The action of
g
on
x
is given by

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto x+(0,0,-t^{-1}b^{\sharp},0)_{-}-t^{-1}b+(0,0,0,-t^{-1})_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto x+(0,0,-t^{-1}b^{\sharp},0)_{-}-t^{-1}T(b,b^{\sharp})x-t^{-1}b+(0,0,t^{-1}b\times b,0)_{-}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+t^{-1}T(b,b^{\sharp})x+(0,0,0,-t^{-1})_{+}+t^{-1}b+(0,0,-t^{-1}b^{\sharp},0)_{-}-t^{-1}N(b)x

\displaystyle=(0,0,0,-t^{-1})_{+}\mapsto(0,0,0,-t^{-1})_{+}.

The action of
g
on
(0,0,c^{\prime},0)_{-}
for an arbitrary
c^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
is given by

\displaystyle(0,0,c^{\prime},0)_{-}

\displaystyle\mapsto(0,0,c^{\prime},0)_{-}+t^{-1}c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp}+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})\big)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(0,0,c^{\prime},0)_{-}+T(b,c^{\prime})x+t^{-1}c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp}+\big(0,0,-t^{-1}(c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp})\times b,0\big)_{-}

\displaystyle\qquad{}-t^{-1}T(c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp},b^{\sharp})x+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})\big)_{+}-t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})b

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\big(0,0,t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})b^{\sharp},0\big)_{-}+T(b,c^{\prime})x

\displaystyle=t^{-1}c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp}-t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})b+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})\big)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto t^{-1}c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp}+\big(0,0,0,t^{-2}T(c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp},b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}-t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})b

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\big(0,0,0,-t^{-2}T(b,c^{\prime})T(b,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})\big)_{+}

\displaystyle=-t^{-1}\big(T(b,c^{\prime})b-c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp}\big)=-t^{-1}U_{b}c^{\prime}

Here we have used twice that
T(c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp},b^{\sharp})=T(c^{\prime},b^{\sharp}\times b^{\sharp})=2tT(c^{\prime},b)
, and also that

-t^{-1}(c^{\prime}\times b^{\sharp})\times b=-c^{\prime}-t^{-1}T(b,c^{\prime})b^{\sharp}

by Axiom
2.3
Item

6
. For arbitrary
c\in J
, the action of
g
on
c
is given by

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\mapsto c+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto c+(0,0,-c\times b,0)_{-}-T(c,b^{\sharp})x+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}-t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})b

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\big(0,0,t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})b^{\sharp},0\big)_{-}+T(c,b^{\sharp})x

\displaystyle=\big(0,0,t^{-1}U_{b^{\sharp}}c,0\big)_{-}+c-t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})b+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(0,0,t^{-1}U_{b^{\sharp}}c,0\big)_{-}+t^{-2}U_{b^{\sharp}}(c)\times b^{\sharp}+\big(0,0,0,t^{-2}T(b,U_{b^{\sharp}}c)\big)_{+}+c

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}-t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})b+\big(0,0,0,-t^{-2}T(c,b^{\sharp})T(b,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(c,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}

\displaystyle=(0,0,t^{-1}U_{b^{\sharp}}c,0)_{-}.

Here we have used that
U_{b^{\sharp}}(c)\times b^{\sharp}=T(b^{\sharp},c)b^{\sharp\sharp}-N(b^{\sharp})c=tT(b^{\sharp},c)b-t^{2}c
by (
3
) in
Lemma

2.19
and
T(b,U_{b^{\sharp}}c)=tT(b^{\sharp},c)
by (
2
) in
Lemma

2.19
(applied to
b^{\sharp}
,
c
in place of
b
,
c^{\prime}
and using that
t=N(b)
is invertible). Finally, the action of
g
on
(0,0,0,1)_{+}
is given by

\displaystyle(0,0,0,1)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(0,0,0,1)_{+}\mapsto(0,0,0,1)_{+}-b+(0,0,b^{\sharp},0)_{-}+tx

\displaystyle\mapsto(0,0,0,1)_{+}-b+\big(0,0,0,-t^{-1}T(b,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}+(0,0,b^{\sharp},0)_{-}+t^{-1}b^{\sharp}\times b^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\big(0,0,0,t^{-1}T(b,b^{\sharp})\big)_{+}+tx+(0,0,-b^{\sharp},0)_{-}-b+(0,0,0,-1)_{+}

\displaystyle=tx.

By
Remark

5.19
, we conclude that
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}=e_{(0,0,-b^{\prime},0)_{+}}\circ e_{(0,b,0,0)_{-}}\circ e_{(0,0,-b^{\prime},0)_{+}}
.
∎

It remains to show that conjugation by
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
induces the desired permutation of the root groups. We first verify this on the short root groups.

Lemma B.18
.

We have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,a,0,0)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{-a^{\iota}}
for all
a\in J
and
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{a^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,(a^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},0,0)_{+}}
for all
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
.

Proof.

It follows from
Lemma

B.13
that

\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,a,0,0)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=L(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},t)^{-1}\circ e_{-a}^{(J^{\prime},J)}\circ L(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},t).

The map on the right-hand side fixes
x
and maps
(\nu,c,c^{\prime},\rho)_{+}
to

\displaystyle\bigl(\nu+T(c^{\iota},a)+tT(a^{\sharp},(c^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}})+t\rho N(a),c+a^{\iota}\times c^{\prime}+t\rho(a^{\sharp})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},\,c^{\prime}+\rho a^{\iota},\,\rho\bigr)

Here, since
(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}})
is a
t
-involution,

\displaystyle T(c^{\iota},a)=T(c,a^{\iota}),\qquad tT\big(a^{\sharp},(c^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}\big)=T\big((a^{\iota})^{\sharp},c^{\prime}\big),

\displaystyle t\rho N(a)=\rho N(a^{\iota}),\qquad t\rho(a^{\sharp})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}=\rho(a^{\iota})^{\sharp}.

The first assertion follows. This implies that

\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{a^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-2}\circ e_{(0,-(a^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},0,0)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{2}

for all
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
, so the second assertion follows from
Remarks

5.19
and
7.7
.
∎

Lemma B.19
.

We have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0,a^{\prime},0)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,-t(a^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},0,0)_{-}}
for all
a^{\prime}\in J
and
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,a,0,0)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,0,-t^{-1}a^{\iota},0)_{+}}
for all
a\in J
.

Proof.

Let
a^{\prime}\in J
. We compute the action of
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0.a^{\prime},0)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
on the generating set

\varphi_{\updownarrow}(kx+L_{1})=(k,0,0,k)_{-}+J^{\prime}+(0,J,0,0)_{+}+ky

of
L
. The module
(0,0,0,k)_{-}
is fixed pointwise and the action on the remaining generators is given as follows, where the final equality for each generator holds because
(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}})
is a
t
-homotopy:

\displaystyle(1,0,0,0)_{-}

\displaystyle\mapsto(1,0,0,0)_{-},

\displaystyle c^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mapsto c^{\prime}+\big(-T((c^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},a^{\prime})t,0,0,0\big)_{-}=c^{\prime}+\big(T((-ta^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},c^{\prime}),0,0,0\big)_{-},

\displaystyle(0,c,0,0)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(0,c,0,0)_{+}-(c^{\iota}\times a^{\prime})^{\iota}+\big(tT((a^{\prime})^{\sharp},c^{\iota}),0,0,0\big)_{-}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{where}\quad tT\big((a^{\prime})^{\sharp},c^{\iota}\big)=T\big(((-ta^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}})^{\sharp},c\big),

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle\mapsto y+\big(0,t(a^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},0,0\big)_{+}-t\big((a^{\prime})^{\sharp}\big)^{\iota}+\big(t^{2}N(a^{\prime}),0,0,0\big)_{-}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{where}\quad-t\big((a^{\prime})^{\sharp}\big)^{\iota}=-\big((-ta^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}\big)^{\sharp},\quad t^{2}N(a^{\prime})=-N\big((-ta^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}\big).

where
c\in J
and
c^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
.
The first assertion follows. This implies that

\displaystyle\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,a,0,0)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-2}\circ e_{(0,0,-t^{-1}a^{\iota},0)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{2}

for all
a\in J
, so the second assertion follows from
Remarks

5.19
and
7.7
.
∎

Lemma B.20
.

We have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{a}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,0,a^{\iota},0)_{-}}
for all
a\in J
and
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0,a^{\prime},0)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{-(a^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}}
for all
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
a\in J
. We compute the action of
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{a}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
on the generating set

\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}(kx+L_{1})=(k,0,0,k)_{-}+kx+(0,J,0,0)_{-}+J^{\prime}

of
L
. The module
(0,0,0,k)_{+}
is fixed pointwise.
The action on the remaining part is given by

\displaystyle\rho x+(0,c,0,0)_{-}+c^{\prime}+(\nu,0,0,0)_{+}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\mapsto(\nu,0,0,0)_{+}+\big(c^{\prime}-\nu a^{\iota}\big)+\big(0,c+t(a\times(c^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}})^{\iota}-t\nu(a^{\sharp})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},0,0\big)_{-}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{}+\big(\rho-T(a,c^{\iota})-tT\big((c^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},a^{\sharp}\big)+t\nu N(a)\big)

for all
\rho,\nu\in k
,
c\in J
and
c^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
. Here, since
(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}})
is a
t
-homotopy,

\displaystyle t\big(a\times(c^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}\big)^{\iota}=a^{\iota}\times c^{\prime},\qquad-t\nu(a^{\sharp})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}=-\nu(a^{\iota})^{\sharp},

\displaystyle-T(a,c^{\iota})=-T(a^{\iota},c),\quad-tT\big((c^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},a^{\sharp}\big)=-T\big((a^{\iota})^{\sharp},c^{\prime}\big),\quad t\nu N(a)=\nu N(a^{\iota}).

The first assertion follows. Hence

\displaystyle\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0,a^{\prime},0)}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-2}\circ e_{(a^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{2}=e_{-(a^{\prime})^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}}}

for all
a^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
by
Remarks

5.19
and
7.7
.
∎

On the long root groups, the required computations are much shorter.

Lemma B.21
.

For all
\nu,\rho\in k
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0,0,\rho)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,0,0,\rho)_{-}}\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(\nu,0,0,0)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(\nu,0,0,0)_{+}}.

Proof.

This follows from
Lemmas

B.17
and
7.6
because the roots
(-1,1)
and
(1,-1)
are adjacent to
(1,1)
and
(-1,-1)
.
∎

Lemma B.22
.

We have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(0,0,0,-t^{-1}\lambda)_{+}}
and
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0,0,\lambda)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{t\lambda x}
for all
\lambda\in k
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in k
. Since
e_{\lambda x}
is the identity on
L_{-1}
,
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
is the identity on
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}(L_{-1})
. Further,
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
acts on
(k,0,0,0)_{-}=\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}(ky)
by

\displaystyle(1,0,0,0)_{-}

\displaystyle\mapsto(1,0,0,0)_{-}+t^{-1}\lambda\zeta+(0,0,0,t^{-2}\lambda^{2})_{+}.

Since
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}(ky+L_{-1})
generates
L
, the first assertion follows. Hence

\displaystyle\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(0,0,0,\lambda)_{+}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-2}\circ e_{-t\lambda x}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{2}=e_{t\lambda x}.

by
Remarks

5.19
and
7.7
.
∎

Lemma B.23
.

We have
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{(-t\lambda,0,0,0)_{-}}
and
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(\lambda,0,0,0)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}=e_{t^{-1}\lambda y}
for all
\lambda\in k
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in k
. Since
e_{\lambda y}
is the identity on
L_{1}
,
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
is the identity on
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}(L_{1})
. Further,
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}
acts on
(0,0,0,k)=\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}(kx)
by

\displaystyle(0,0,0,1)_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto(0,0,0,1)-t\lambda\zeta+(t^{2}\lambda^{2},0,0,0)_{-}.

Since
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}(ky+L_{-1})
generates
L
, the first assertion follows. Thus

\displaystyle\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-1}\circ e_{(\lambda,0,0,0)_{-}}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{-2}\circ e_{-t^{-1}\lambda y}\circ\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{2}=e_{t^{-1}\lambda y}

by
Remarks

5.19
and
7.7
.
∎

Proposition B.24
.

Assume that there exists
b\in J
such that
N(b)
is invertible and define
(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},t)
as in
B.16
. Then
\varphi_{\operatorname{\rotatebox{120.0}{\!${}_{\leftrightarrow}$}}}^{(\iota,{\mathord{\mathchoice{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\iota$}}}{\reflectbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\iota$}}}}},t)}
is a
(-1,-1)
-Weyl element with respect to
(U_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in G_{2}}
.

Proof.

This follows from
Lemmas

B.17
,
B.21
,
B.22
,
B.23
,
B.18
,
B.19
and
B.20
.
∎

Proposition B.25
.

Assume that there exists
p\in J
such that
N(p)
is invertible. Then for all
\alpha\in G_{2}
, there exists an
\alpha
-Weyl element with respect to
(U_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in G_{2}}
.

Proof.

By
Propositions

B.24
and
B.15
, there exist Weyl elements for the system
\{(2,1),(-1,-1)\}
of simple roots in
G_{2}
. The assertion now follows from
Remark

1.8
.
∎
